<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the Unique Games Conjecture. For every fixed $`\varepsilon,\delta\in(0,1/2)`$, we give a deterministic polynomial-time reduction from 3SAT to Unique Games over a fixed finite alphabet, with completeness at least $`1-\varepsilon`$ and soundness at most <i>δ</i>.

- **claim-unique-games-reduction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-ugc)): For every fixed $\varepsilon,\delta\in(0,1/2)$, there are an integer $s\ge1$ and a deterministic polynomial-time reduction from $\mathrm{3SAT}$ to explicit unweighted Unique Games instances $G_\varphi$ over $K=\mathbb F_2^s$ such that $$\begin{aligned} \varphi\text{ satisfiable} &\quad\Longrightarrow\quad \mathop{\mathrm{val}}(G_\varphi)\ge1-\varepsilon,\\ \varphi\text{ unsatisfiable} &\quad\Longrightarrow\quad \mathop{\mathrm{val}}(G_\varphi)\le\delta. \end{aligned}$$ The graph is simple and bipartite, and every constraint is a translation $a(v_e)=a(u_e)+c_e$ of $K$. The alphabet, the degree of the running-time polynomial, and its constants depend only on $\varepsilon,\delta$.
  - Explanation: The stated reduction produces explicit unweighted simple bipartite translation games with independently prescribed completeness and soundness errors. Its alphabet and polynomial depend on the fixed errors.
- **claim-latent-alphabet-separation** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-latent)): For every $p_*>0$ there is an integer $r_*$ such that, for every sufficiently large integer $\ell$, the following objects exist: a finite-dimensional binary space $\mathcal V$ containing an identified subspace $K=\mathbb F_2^\ell$, a map $C:\mathcal V\to K$, and a rational probability distribution $\mu$ on $\mathcal V$. They satisfy $$\label{eq:latent-equivariance} C(x+k)=C(x)+k \qquad(x\in\mathcal V,\ k\in K),$$ and, for independent uniform $x\in\mathcal V$ and $a\sim\mu$, $$\label{eq:latent-small-change} \Pr[C(x+a)\ne C(x)]\le p_*.$$ Moreover, every linear subspace $G\le\mathcal V^*$ with $\dim(G\|_K)\ge r_*$ satisfies $$\label{eq:latent-detection} \Pr_{a\sim\mu}[\text{there exists }g\in G\text{ with }g(a)\ne0] \ge\frac18.$$ In particular, $r_*$ is fixed before $\ell$ is chosen. For rational $p_*$, the threshold and all the finite data can be found by a terminating deterministic algorithm.
  - Explanation: The gadget makes its nonlinear output stable under additive noise while ensuring detection by linear observation families of sufficiently large restriction rank. Rational error budgets admit a terminating deterministic construction.
- **claim-quadratic-update-spaces** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-latent-block)): For every line $A$, the space $U_A$ is binary linear of dimension $b$. For $a\in A$ and $x\in X$, one has $D(x,a)\in D_A$; for $a,a'\in A$, one has $D(a,a')=0$. The trace annihilator of $D_A$ in $B^*=F^3$ is $A$, and the spaces $U_A$ together span $X\oplus B$.
  - Explanation: The block updates form binary linear spaces. Their polar terms lie in the prescribed annihilating hyperplanes, and the update spaces collectively span the block input.
- **claim-block-noise-response** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-latent-block-noise)): Fix a line $A$. For independent uniform $(x,y)\in X\oplus B$ and $\zeta\in U_A\setminus\{0\}$, $$\Pr\bigl[C_{\mathrm{blk}}((x,y)+\zeta) \ne C_{\mathrm{blk}}(x,y)\bigr]=1-\theta.$$
  - Explanation: The exact change probability averages over an independent uniform aggregate and a uniform nonzero update in the chosen block space.
- **claim-block-restriction-kernel** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-latent-block-kernel)): Let $S\le B^*$ and let $\gamma:S\to X^*$ be binary linear. For each line $A$, define $$\kappa_{A,\gamma}:S\longrightarrow U_A^*, \qquad \kappa_{A,\gamma}(z)=(\gamma_z,z)\|_{U_A}.$$ Under the trace identification, write $\gamma_z(x)=\operatorname{Tr}(g(z)\cdot x)$ for the associated binary linear map $g:S\to F^3$. For every nonzero $z\in S$, $$\kappa_{A,\gamma}(z)=0 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad A=Fz \quad\text{and}\quad z\cdot g(z)=\sqrt{z_1z_2z_3}.$$
  - Explanation: The restriction kernel is characterized by membership in the character's field line and the trace alignment equation for the chosen linear extension.
- **claim-generic-character-spaces-abundant** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-latent-generic)): For each fixed $r\ge1$, a uniformly random binary $r$-dimensional subspace of $F^3$ is generic with probability tending to one as $d\to\infty$.
  - Explanation: At each fixed binary rank, uniform character spaces satisfy the genericity conditions with probability tending to certainty as the field dimension grows.
- **claim-recursive-noise-change** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-latent-noise)): For independent uniform $u\in P_t$ and noise $\xi_t$, $$\label{eq:latent-change-probability} p_t:=\Pr[C_t(u+\xi_t)\ne C_t(u)] =(1-q^{-3})(1-\theta)^t.$$
  - Explanation: The probability of a nonlinear output change decreases geometrically with recursion height. The observable consequently becomes increasingly stable.
- **claim-lift-child-factorization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-latent-lift-factorization)): Let a lift with domain $S$ be given on $P_t$, where $t\ge1$. For each child $(A,J)$, restriction to that child’s shift space factors through $\lambda_{t-1}$. The resulting characters on the child output space $B$ have the form $$\label{eq:latent-child-characters} \phi_{AJ}(z)=J^*\bigl((\gamma_z,z)\|_{U_A}\bigr)\in B^*,$$ where $\gamma:S\to X^*$ is a single binary linear map common to all the children.
  - Explanation: Every parent lift induces child output characters through a common linear extension map. This common map permits uniform rank-loss estimates across outgoing branches.
- **claim-full-lift-noise-detection** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-latent-full-lift-detection)): Set $$\label{eq:latent-depth} s=\left\lfloor\frac{H_{r_0}}{8\theta}\right\rfloor.$$ Every full lift on $P_s$ detects $\xi_s$ with probability at least $1/4$: $$\label{eq:latent-full-lift-detection} \Pr[\text{there exists }z\in B^*\text{ with }\psi_z(\xi_s)\ne0] \ge\frac14.$$
  - Explanation: At the prescribed recursion height, every full lift retains a positive noise detection probability. Harmonic rank control bounds extinction before the final uniform leaf symbol.
- **claim-weighted-parity-gap** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-parity)): For every fixed rational $\xi>0$, there is a deterministic polynomial-time reduction from Boolean satisfiability to weighted parity instances, with binary-encoded rational weights, such that $$\begin{array}{ll} \textup{YES:}&\mathop{\mathrm{OPT}}(\mathcal E)\ge 1-\xi,\\ \textup{NO:}&\mathop{\mathrm{OPT}}(\mathcal E)\le \frac12+\xi. \end{array}$$ The running-time polynomial may depend on $\xi$.
  - Explanation: The imported parity reduction supplies a nearly satisfiable weighted instance in the satisfiable case and a bounded optimum in the unsatisfiable case, with rational weights and fixed-error polynomial complexity.
- **claim-distinct-parity-positions** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-distinct-parity)): From a weighted parity instance $\mathcal E$ one can deterministically construct, in polynomial time, an instance $\mathcal E'$ in which the three variable IDs of every occurrence are distinct. Its weights are positive rationals of polynomial bit length, and $$\mathop{\mathrm{OPT}}(\mathcal E')\ge\mathop{\mathrm{OPT}}(\mathcal E), \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{OPT}}(\mathcal E') \le \frac{1+\mathop{\mathrm{OPT}}(\mathcal E)}2+\frac3{100}.$$ In particular, Theorem <a href="#thm:parity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:parity">11</a> with $\xi<1/100$, followed by this preprocessing, gives completeness at least $1-\xi$ and NO optimum at most $4/5$.
  - Explanation: Variable cloning removes repeated variable IDs within each parity occurrence, preserves completeness, and controls the increase in optimum using an explicit collision allowance.
- **claim-outer-matrix-completeness** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-outer-completeness)): If $\mathcal E$ has an assignment of value at least $1-\xi$, and the latent map satisfies $$\Pr_{x\text{ uniform in }\mathcal V,\ a\sim\mu} [C(x+a)\ne C(x)]\le p_*,$$ then $$\mathop{\mathrm{val}}(\mathcal G)\ge 1-k\xi-\frac{p_*}{2}.$$
  - Explanation: A nearly satisfying global parity assignment defines consistent evaluations on reduced keys. Tuple failures and latent output changes account for the completeness loss.
- **claim-shortcode-slice-agreement** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-shortcode)): For every $\eta\in(0,1)$ there are $\alpha\in(0,1]$, an integer $r_{\rm s}\ge1$, and an integer $\ell_0$, depending only on $\eta$, with the following property. For every $\ell\ge\ell_0$ there is $m_0=m_0(\eta,\ell)$ such that, for every $m\ge m_0$ and every function $f:\operatorname{Mat}_{\ell,m}\to\mathbb F_2^\ell$, if $$\Pr_{M,a,l}\bigl[f(M)=f(M+a l^\top)\bigr]\ge\eta, \label{eq:shortcode-acceptance}$$ then some slice $\mathcal S$ as in Equation <a href="#eq:shortcode-slice" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:shortcode-slice">[eq:shortcode-slice]</a>, with $n_r,n_c\le r_{\rm s}$, and some $z\in\mathbb F_2^m$, $u\in\mathbb F_2^\ell$ satisfy $$\Pr_{M\mid\mathcal S}[f(M)=Mz+u]\ge\alpha. \label{eq:shortcode-conclusion}$$ Here $M$, $a\in\mathbb F_2^\ell$, and $l\in\mathbb F_2^m$ are independent and uniform, and $M\mid\mathcal S$ is uniform on the slice. No folding condition is imposed on $f$.
  - Explanation: Sufficient equality acceptance under ordinary uniform rank-one noise implies agreement with an affine evaluation on a nonempty slice with bounded row and column specifications. Folding is unnecessary for this inverse statement.
- **claim-matrix-advice-decoding** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-matrix-decoding)): Choose the latent and shortcode parameters in the order specified by Equation <a href="#eq:matrix-parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:matrix-parameters">[eq:matrix-parameters]</a>, fixing $\mathcal V=K\oplus W$ before $k$. There are a constant $\gamma>0$ and an integer $k_0\ge8$, depending only on these fixed parameters, with the following property.* *For every cube $k\ge k_0$ and every parity instance, suppose a function $$F_Q:\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(L_Q,\mathcal V)\longrightarrow K$$ is given for every $Q\in\mathcal Q_k$. Assume folding for every such $Q$, every $P\in\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(L_Q,\mathcal V)$, and every $c\in K$, and assume sharing for every permitted projection $\bar\pi:L_U\to L_O$ and every $P'\in\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(L_O,\mathcal V)$: $$\begin{aligned} F_Q(P+c e_0^\top)&=F_Q(P)+c,\\ F_U(P'\bar\pi)&=F_O(P'). \end{aligned} \label{eq:matrix-family-identities}$$ If this family passes the table test of Equation <a href="#eq:matrix-test" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:matrix-test">[eq:matrix-test]</a> with probability at least $0.99$, then the projected advice experiment with $\beta=k^{-2/3}$ has local strategies whose probability of full answer agreement is at least $\gamma$.
  - Explanation: High latent-test acceptance, folding, and sharing yield local advice strategies with a positive full-answer agreement probability independent of the growing tuple length and parity instance.
- **claim-matrix-spectral-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-matrix-spectral)): If the table family passes Equation <a href="#eq:matrix-test" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:matrix-test">[eq:matrix-test]</a> with probability at least $0.99$, then $$\mathbb E_{U,T}\Pr_{M,a,l} [F_U(M,T)=F_U(M+a l^\top,T)] \ge 0.9\,2^{-r_*}, \label{eq:surrogate-acceptance}$$ where $a$ is uniform in $K$ and the other matrix and vector samples are uniform in their respective spaces. Consequently at least $10\eta$ of the pairs $(U,T)$ have the inner probability at least $4\eta$.
  - Explanation: Latent detection limits the contribution of frequencies with large restriction rank. The remaining Fourier mass gives positive ordinary-shortcode acceptance on a positive fraction of table fibers.
- **claim-good-row-fibers-and-answer-sets** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-good-row-advice)): For all sufficiently large $k$, a table family with matrix-test acceptance at least $0.99$ satisfies $$\Pr_{U,T,A,M}[(U,T,A,AM)\text{ is good}] \ge g_0, \qquad g_0=10\eta^2\,2^{-\ell r_{\rm s}}>0. \label{eq:good-advice-mass}$$ The lower bound and the required largeness of $k$ are independent of the parity instance and the family.* *For every positive integer $k$ and every family satisfying Equation <a href="#eq:matrix-family-identities" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:matrix-family-identities">[eq:matrix-family-identities]</a>, one can select deterministically for each good row advice at most $r_{\rm s}$ column specifications $Mq_j=t_j$, with $q_j\in L_U$ and $t_j\in K$, and a target $Mz+u$, with $z\in L_U$, $u\in K$, and $e_0^\top z=1$, satisfying the definition of goodness. Writing $Z=\mathop{\mathrm{span}}\{q_j\}$, the chosen witness determines the answer set $$\mathcal A=(z+Z)\cap\{v\in L_U:e_0^\top v=1\}, \qquad 1\le \|\mathcal A\|\le 2^{r_{\rm s}}. \label{eq:row-answer-set}$$ The witness and $\mathcal A$ depend only on $(U,T,A,S_0)$ and the fixed table family. The slice and its target retain the agreement guarantee in the definition of goodness.
  - Explanation: An erasure argument ensures a positive mass of good row advice. For each good datum, folding supplies a deterministically selected witness whose target coset contains a short nonempty set of actual answers.
- **claim-padded-table-uniformity** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-projected-tv)): Conditional on every $U$, the distribution of $$(M,T)=(M_O\bar\pi,T_O\bar\pi)$$ has total variation distance $o(1)$ from the unrestricted uniform distribution on $\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(L_U,K)\times\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(L_U,W)$, as $k$ tends to infinity along cubes. The bound is uniform in $U$ and the parity instance. The projection choices are averaged out in this comparison.
  - Explanation: After averaging the sparse projection choices, the full padded table law approaches unrestricted uniform sampling. The comparison includes the intercept and is uniform over questions and instances.
- **claim-chosen-witness-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-projected-witness)): Suppose the matrix-test acceptance is at least $0.99$. For all sufficiently large cubes $k$, a set of probability at least $$\gamma_1=\frac{\alpha g_0}{8}\,2^{-\ell r_{\rm s}}>0 \label{eq:projected-witness-mass}$$ of the joint data $D$ in Definition <a href="#def:projected-advice" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:projected-advice">15</a> has the following properties. The padded $U$-advice is good, with the chosen answer set $\mathcal A$ and its certifying witness. Writing $z'=\bar\pi z$, that witness transfers to a nonempty slice $$AM_O=S_O,\qquad M_O\bar\pi q_j=t_j,$$ on which $$\Pr\bigl[F_O(M_O,T_O)=M_Oz'+u\mid AM_O=S_O,\ M_O\bar\pi q_j=t_j\text{ for all }j\bigr] \ge\alpha/4. \label{eq:transferred-witness}$$ The probability in this equation is taken over a uniform matrix in the specified slice.
  - Explanation: A positive fraction of projected joint data preserves the chosen good-advice witness on a nonempty slice with positive target agreement. The witness is selected from visible advice before this transfer analysis.
- **claim-fourier-coset-agreement** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-fourier-coset-decoding)): Fix joint data satisfying Lemma <a href="#lem:projected-witness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:projected-witness">20</a>. Conditional on these data, the two rules just defined, using independent randomness, agree under $\bar\pi$ with probability at least $$2^{-\ell r_{\rm s}}(\alpha/8)^2\,2^{-r_{\rm s}}. \label{eq:conditional-decoding}$$
  - Explanation: The transferred slice forces squared Fourier mass into a target coset. Contraction produces a projected actual answer, and the independent sample from the short answer set supplies full agreement.
- **claim-alphabet-independent-repetition** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-repetition)): Let $G$ be a finite bipartite projection game, with an arbitrary probability distribution on its constraint occurrences. Question sets and answer alphabets may be arbitrary finite sets, and parallel constraint occurrences are allowed. The game $G^{\otimes n}$ samples $n$ independent occurrences, sends each prover its tuple of questions, and accepts if all $n$ projection constraints hold. For every integer $n\ge1$ and every $0<g<1$, $$\mathop{\mathrm{val}}(G)\le 1-g \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \mathop{\mathrm{val}}(G^{\otimes n})\le (1-g^2/16)^n. \label{eq:projection-repetition}$$ The bound is independent of the question-set and alphabet sizes.
  - Explanation: The classical independent parallel product decreases projection-game value exponentially under a fixed base gap, uniformly over finite question and answer alphabets and occurrence distributions.
- **claim-clean-coordinate-agreement-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-clean)): If $\mathop{\mathrm{OPT}}(\mathcal E)\le 4/5$, every pair of local randomized strategies in the advice experiment satisfies $$\Pr\bigl[\bar\pi(a_U)=a_O\bigr] \le \left(1-\frac{\beta\,2^{-\dim W-r_{\rm s}}}{3600}\right)^k \le \exp\!\left( -\frac{2^{-\dim W-r_{\rm s}}}{3600}\,k^{1/3} \right). \label{eq:clean-agreement-bound}$$ In particular, the upper bound tends to zero uniformly over the parity instance as $k$ tends to infinity along cubes, with the advice spaces fixed.
  - Explanation: On the stated parity NO regime, clean singleton coordinates have vanishing advice slopes. Local reconstruction and projection-game repetition bound every pair of advice strategies by a quantity tending to zero.
- **claim-matrix-game-gap** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-matrix-gap)): Fix $p_*>0$ and choose the latent and shortcode data in the order of Equation <a href="#eq:matrix-parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:matrix-parameters">[eq:matrix-parameters]</a>. For every sufficiently large cube $k$, the matrix game $\mathcal G$ constructed from a weighted parity instance $\mathcal E$ with distinct positions satisfies $$\begin{aligned} \mathop{\mathrm{OPT}}(\mathcal E)\ge1-\xi &\quad\Longrightarrow\quad \mathop{\mathrm{val}}(\mathcal G)\ge1-k\xi-p_*/2,\\ \mathop{\mathrm{OPT}}(\mathcal E)\le4/5 &\quad\Longrightarrow\quad \mathop{\mathrm{val}}(\mathcal G)\le99/100. \end{aligned}$$ The lower threshold on $k$ depends only on the fixed latent and shortcode data, not on $\mathcal E$ or $\xi$.
  - Explanation: The decoded agreement lower bound contradicts the clean-coordinate upper bound for sufficiently large cubes. This yields a fixed soundness gap alongside adjustable completeness.
- **claim-rounding-and-subdivision** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-unweighted-subdivision)): Let $H$ be a finite permutation-constraint game with $h\ge1$ positive rational occurrence weights summing to one. For every positive rational $\tau$, put $Q=\lceil h/\tau\rceil$. There is a deterministic construction of an unweighted simple bipartite game $B$ with $4Q$ edges and the same alphabet such that $$\left\|\mathop{\mathrm{val}}(B)-\left(1-\frac{1-\mathop{\mathrm{val}}(H)}4\right)\right\| \le\frac{\tau}{4}. \label{eq:rounded-subdivision-value}$$ If the constraints of $H$ are translations on a finite binary vector space, so are those of $B$. For fixed $\tau$, when $H$ is listed by its vertices, occurrences, permutation tables, and binary rational weights, the construction takes polynomial time in its encoding length.
  - Explanation: Largest-remainder rounding converts rational occurrence weights into multiplicities. Fresh path interiors then produce a simple bipartite game, with a controlled value transformation and preserved binary translations.
- **claim-fixed-csp-gap-transfer** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cor-raghavendra-consequence)): Let $J$ be a fixed $\Lambda$-instance with $A=\operatorname{OPT}(J)<B=\operatorname{SDP}_{R}(J)$. For every fixed $0<\eta<(B-A)/2$, it is NP-hard to distinguish $\Lambda$-instances $I$ satisfying $$\operatorname{OPT}(I)\ge B-\eta \qquad\text{from those satisfying}\qquad \operatorname{OPT}(I)\le A+\eta.$$
  - Explanation: A fixed gap between integral and relaxation values becomes an NP-hard distinction between rationally weighted instances near those respective values.
- **claim-ordering-csp-threshold** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-ordering-csp)): Fix $k\ge2$ and $\varnothing\ne\Pi\subsetneq S_k$, and put $\rho_\Pi=\|\Pi\|/k!$. For every fixed $0<\varepsilon<(1-\rho_\Pi)/2$, it is NP-hard to distinguish finite weighted $\Pi$-ordering-CSP instances $I$ satisfying $$\mathop{\mathrm{OPT}}_\Pi(I)\ge1-\varepsilon \qquad\text{from those satisfying}\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{OPT}}_\Pi(I)\le\rho_\Pi+\varepsilon.$$ Consequently, approximation at every fixed ratio $r\in(\rho_\Pi,1]$ is NP-hard. Together with the random-ordering guarantee, this gives the optimal threshold $\rho_\Pi$.
  - Explanation: The finite weighted ordering model has an NP-hard gap between near-perfect value and the random-order baseline. Its strict approximation hardness matches the deterministic random-order guarantee.
- **claim-cut-deletion-clustering-hardness** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-cut-clustering)): For every fixed real constant $C>1$, it is NP-hard to approximate each of the four minimization problems above within factor $C$. For <span class="smallcaps">Sparsest Cut</span>, this is hardness of the search task under polynomial-time Cook reductions: the algorithm must output a cutset, not only an estimate of the optimum. Consequently, unless $\mathrm P=\mathrm{NP}$, none of these problems admits a deterministic polynomial-time approximation with any fixed constant factor.
  - Explanation: The stated minimization models inherit hardness for every fixed constant approximation factor. Sparsest Cut carries the explicit cutset-output search qualification under Cook reductions.
- **claim-simple-strong-left-games** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-simple-strong-left)): Fix $0<\zeta,\nu<1$ and integers $d,H\ge1$. There are integers $h,R\ge1$, with $h\ge H$ and $d\mid h$, and a deterministic polynomial-time reduction from $\mathrm{3SAT}$ to nonempty explicit unweighted simple bipartite Unique Games instances $\Phi_\varphi=(A,B,E)$ over $[R]$ with the following properties. Every left vertex has degree $h$. If $\varphi$ is satisfiable, one labeling strongly satisfies at least a $1-\zeta$ fraction of $A$. If $\varphi$ is unsatisfiable, then $\mathop{\mathrm{val}}(\Phi_\varphi)<\nu$. The degree $h$ and the two errors used in Theorem <a href="#thm:ugc" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:ugc">1</a> may be chosen from $\zeta,\nu,d,H$ before that theorem supplies the alphabet $R$.
  - Explanation: Normalization, tagged right copies, and restricted occurrence sequences give simple left-regular games with strong completeness. Degree requirements and game errors precede the alphabet choice.
- **claim-finite-ordering-transfer** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-finite-ordering-transfer)): Fix $k\ge2$ and $\varnothing\ne\Pi\subsetneq S_k$, put $\rho_\Pi=\|\Pi\|/k!$, and fix $0<\delta<(1-\rho_\Pi)/2$. There are integers $q,H\ge1$ and positive rationals $\zeta,\nu<1$, depending only on $k,\Pi,\delta$, with the following property for every fixed integer $R\ge1$.* *Let $\Phi=(A,B,E)$ be a nonempty explicit unweighted simple bipartite Unique Games instance over $[R]$, oriented from $A$ to $B$, whose common left degree $h$ satisfies $h\ge H$ and $q\mid h$. There is a deterministic transformation, polynomial in the explicit size of $\Phi$ for fixed $R$, producing an explicit list of $\Pi$-ordering constraints on distinct variables within each constraint, with nonnegative rational weights, each encoded by a binary numerator and a positive binary denominator, summing to one. If some labeling strongly satisfies at least $(1-\zeta)\|A\|$ left vertices, then $\mathop{\mathrm{OPT}}_\Pi(I)\ge1-\delta$. If $\mathop{\mathrm{val}}(\Phi)\le\nu$, then $\mathop{\mathrm{OPT}}_\Pi(I)\le\rho_\Pi+\delta$. The constants $q,H,\zeta,\nu$ are independent of both $R$ and $h$.
  - Explanation: The transformation emits an explicit rational occurrence list on distinct variables. Strong-left completeness and low edge value transfer to an ordering gap with constants chosen independently of alphabet and left degree.
- **claim-rational-local-ordering-data** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-ordering-local-data)): For every integer $q\ge1$ and rational $\eta>0$, there are a finite local instance $J=(U,\mathcal C,\omega)$, with $\|U\|=m\ge2$, and probability laws $\mu_c$ on $[m]^k$ with the following properties. Every tuple $c$ has $k$ distinct variables; the weights $\omega_c$ and all atoms of $\mu_c$ are rational. Every one-position marginal of $\mu_c$ is uniform on $[m]$, and there is a fixed $0<\alpha\le1/2$ such that $\mu_c(x)>\alpha$ for every $c,x$. Moreover $$\operatorname{opt}_q(J)\le\rho_\Pi+\eta,\qquad v_{\mathrm{loc}}:= \sum_c\omega_c\mathbb E_{X\sim\mu_c}\overline{P_0}(X) \ge1-\eta. \label{eq:ordering-local-gap}$$ The local laws have common feasible Gram data for the relaxation in (Guruswami et al. 2011, sec. 8.3) [[GuruswamiEtAl2011OrderingCSP](#ref-GuruswamiEtAl2011OrderingCSP)]: there are a unit vector $I$ and vectors $b_{u,j}$, $u\in U,j\in[m]$, with $$\sum_j b_{u,j}=I,\quad \langle b_{u,i},b_{u,j}\rangle=0\ (i\ne j),\quad \\|b_{u,j}\\|^2=\frac1m,$$ and, for every two positions $r,s$ of $c$, $$\langle b_{u_{c,r},i},b_{u_{c,s},j}\rangle =\Pr_{X\sim\mu_c}[X_r=i,\ X_s=j]. \label{eq:ordering-local-moments}$$ All these data are fixed by $k,\Pi,q,\eta$.
  - Explanation: Finite-copy smoothing yields rational full-support local laws with uniform marginals and common feasible Gram data, retaining a local ordering gap.
- **claim-uniform-ordering-collision** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-ordering-collision)): For every $0<\kappa<1/2$, there is $\mu_0(\kappa)>0$. For each $m\ge2$, there is a nonnegative function $r_c^{m,\kappa}(t)\to0$ as $t\downarrow0$ with the following property, uniformly over all $R\ge1$. Let $F,G:[m]^R\to[0,1]$ have the same mean $0<\mu\le\mu_0(\kappa)$ and satisfy $$\operatorname{Inf}_\ell(T_{1-\kappa}F)\le t,\qquad \operatorname{Inf}_\ell(T_{1-\kappa}G)\le t \quad(\ell\in[R]).$$ For every coupling $(Z,Z')$ whose two marginals are uniform on $[m]^R$, $$\mathbb E\bigl[ (T_{1-2\kappa}F)(Z)(T_{1-2\kappa}G)(Z')\bigr] \le \mu^{1+\kappa/2}+r_c^{m,\kappa}(t). \label{eq:ordering-collision}$$ The function $r_c^{m,\kappa}$ is independent of $R,\mu,F,G$ and the coupling.
  - Explanation: Equal small means and low smoothed influences bound the product expectation under every coupling with uniform product marginals. The error is uniform in product dimension and the coupling.
- **claim-simplex-local-rounding** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-ordering-rounding)): Let $J,\mu_c,\alpha$ be the data of Lemma <a href="#lem:ordering-local-data" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:ordering-local-data">31</a>, and fix $0<\lambda<1$. For an integer $R\ge1$, choose $c$ with probability $\omega_c$. Conditional on this one choice of $c$, choose the $R$ coordinate tuples independently from $\mu_c$, producing strings $Z_1,\ldots,Z_k\in[m]^R$. Then obtain $\widetilde Z_i$ by independently resampling every position and coordinate uniformly with probability $\lambda$. For a single function $F=(F^1,\ldots,F^q):[m]^R\to\mathcal S_q$, used for every $c$ and every position, suppose that $0<t<1/(kq)$ and $$\operatorname{Inf}_\ell(T_{1-\lambda}F^j)\le t \quad(\ell\in[R],\ j\in[q]).$$ Then $$\mathbb E_{c,Z,\widetilde Z} P_0^{\mathrm{ml}}\bigl(F(\widetilde Z_1),\ldots, F(\widetilde Z_k)\bigr) \le \operatorname{opt}_q(J)+r_s(kqt), \label{eq:ordering-rounding}$$ where $r_s(u)\to0$ as $u\downarrow0$, independently of $R$. The function $r_s$ depends only on the fixed local data, $k,q,\lambda$.
  - Explanation: A single simplex-valued function shared across local occurrences and positions has bounded noisy payoff under the stated influence assumptions. Common Gram data permit comparison with a random assignment of the local instance.

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-distinct-parity",
        "exact_quote": "From a weighted parity instance $\\mathcal E$ one can deterministically construct, in polynomial time, an instance $\\mathcal E'$ in which the three variable IDs of every occurrence are distinct.       "id": "claim-outer-matrix-completeness",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-shortcode",
        "exact_quote": "For every $\\eta\\in(0,1)$ there are $\\alpha\\in(0,1]$, an integer $r_{\\rm s}\\ge1$, and an integer $\\ell_0$, depending only on $\\eta$, with the following property. For every $\\ell\\ge\\ell_0$ there is $m_0=m_0(\\eta,\\ell)$ such that, for every $m\\ge m_0$ and every function $f:\\operatorname{Mat}_{\\ell,m}\\to\\mathbb F_2^\\ell$, if $$\\Pr_{M,a,l}\\bigl[f(M)=f(M+a l^\\top)\\bigr]\\ge\\eta,\n \\label{eq:shortcode-acceptance}$$ then some slice $\\mathcal S$ as in Equation <a href=\"#eq:shortcode-slice\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:shortcode-slice\">[eq:shortcode-slice]</a>, with $n_r,n_c\\le r_{\\rm s}$, and some $z\\in\\mathbb F_2^m$, $u\\in\\mathbb F_2^\\ell$ satisfy $$\\Pr_{M\\mid\\mathcal S}[f(M)=Mz+u]\\ge\\alpha.\n \\label{eq:shortcode-conclusion}$$ Here $M$, $a\\in\\mathbb F_2^\\ell$, and $l\\in\\mathbb F_2^m$ are independent and uniform, and $M\\mid\\mathcal S$ is uniform on the slice. No folding condition is imposed on $f$.",
        "source_id": "PM-102.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-matrix-decoding",
        "exact_quote": "Choose the latent and shortcode parameters in the order specified by Equation <a href=\"#eq:matrix-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:matrix-parameters\">[eq:matrix-parameters]</a>, fixing $\\mathcal V=K\\oplus W$ before $k$. There are a constant $\\gamma>0$ and an integer $k_0\\ge8$, depending only on these fixed parameters, with the following property.*\n\n\n*For every cube $k\\ge k_0$ and every parity instance, suppose a function $$F_Q:\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(L_Q,\\mathcal V)\\longrightarrow K$$ is given for every $Q\\in\\mathcal Q_k$. Assume folding for every such $Q$, every $P\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(L_Q,\\mathcal V)$, and every $c\\in K$, and assume sharing for every permitted projection $\\bar\\pi:L_U\\to L_O$ and every $P'\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(L_O,\\mathcal V)$: $$\\begin{aligned}\n F_Q(P+c e_0^\\top)&=F_Q(P)+c,\\\\\n F_U(P'\\bar\\pi)&=F_O(P').\n \\end{aligned}\n \\label{eq:matrix-family-identities}$$ If this family passes the table test of Equation <a href=\"#eq:matrix-test\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:matrix-test\">[eq:matrix-test]</a> with probability at least $0.99$, then the projected advice experiment with $\\beta=k^{-2/3}$ has local strategies whose probability of full answer agreement is at least $\\gamma$.",
        "source_id": "PM-102.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-good-row-advice",
        "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently large $k$, a table family with matrix-test acceptance at least $0.99$ satisfies $$\\Pr_{U,T,A,M}[(U,T,A,AM)\\text{ is good}]\n \\ge g_0,\n \\qquad\n g_0=10\\eta^2\\,2^{-\\ell r_{\\rm s}}>0.\n \\label{eq:good-advice-mass}$$ The lower bound and the required largeness of $k$ are independent of the parity instance and the family.*\n\n*For every positive integer $k$ and every family satisfying Equation <a href=\"#eq:matrix-family-identities\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:matrix-family-identities\">[eq:matrix-family-identities]</a>, one can select deterministically for each good row advice at most $r_{\\rm s}$ column specifications $Mq_j=t_j$, with $q_j\\in L_U$ and $t_j\\in K$, and a target $Mz+u$, with $z\\in L_U$, $u\\in K$, and $e_0^\\top z=1$, satisfying the definition of goodness. Writing $Z=\\mathop{\\mathrm{span}}\\{q_j\\}$, the chosen witness determines the answer set $$\\mathcal A=(z+Z)\\cap\\{v\\in L_U:e_0^\\top v=1\\},\n \\qquad 1\\le |\\mathcal A|\\le 2^{r_{\\rm s}}.\n \\label{eq:row-answer-set}$$ The witness and $\\mathcal A$ depend only on $(U,T,A,S_0)$ and the fixed table family. The slice and its target retain the agreement guarantee in the definition of goodness.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-repetition",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be a finite bipartite projection game, with an arbitrary probability distribution on its constraint occurrences. Question sets and answer alphabets may be arbitrary finite sets, and parallel constraint occurrences are allowed. The game $G^{\\otimes n}$ samples $n$ independent occurrences, sends each prover its tuple of questions, and accepts if all $n$ projection constraints hold. For every integer $n\\ge1$ and every $0<g<1$, $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{val}}(G)\\le 1-g\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{val}}(G^{\\otimes n})\\le (1-g^2/16)^n.\n \\label{eq:projection-repetition}$$ The bound is independent of the question-set and alphabet sizes.",
        "source_id": "PM-102.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-repetition",
          "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be a finite bipartite projection game, with an arbitrary probability distribution on its constraint occurrences.           "source_id": "PM-102.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $G$ be a finite bipartite projection game, with an arbitrary probability distribution on its constraint occurrences.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-unweighted-subdivision",
        "exact_quote": "Let $H$ be a finite permutation-constraint game with $h\\ge1$ positive rational occurrence weights summing to one. For every positive rational $\\tau$, put $Q=\\lceil h/\\tau\\rceil$. There is a deterministic construction of an unweighted simple bipartite game $B$ with $4Q$ edges and the same alphabet such that $$\\left|\\mathop{\\mathrm{val}}(B)-\\left(1-\\frac{1-\\mathop{\\mathrm{val}}(H)}4\\right)\\right|\n \\le\\frac{\\tau}{4}.\n \\label{eq:rounded-subdivision-value}$$ If the constraints of $H$ are translations on a finite binary vector space, so are those of $B$. For fixed $\\tau$, when $H$ is listed by its vertices, occurrences, permutation tables, and binary rational weights, the construction takes polynomial time in its encoding length.",
        "source_id": "PM-102.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-cut-clustering",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed real constant $C>1$, it is NP-hard to approximate each of the four minimization problems above within factor $C$. For <span class=\"smallcaps\">Sparsest Cut</span>, this is hardness of the search task under polynomial-time Cook reductions: the algorithm must output a cutset, not only an estimate of the optimum.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-simple-strong-left",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $0<\\zeta,\\nu<1$ and integers $d,H\\ge1$. There are integers $h,R\\ge1$, with $h\\ge H$ and $d\\mid h$, and a deterministic polynomial-time reduction from $\\mathrm{3SAT}$ to nonempty explicit unweighted simple bipartite Unique Games instances $\\Phi_\\varphi=(A,B,E)$ over $[R]$ with the following properties. Every left vertex has degree $h$. If $\\varphi$ is satisfiable, one labeling strongly satisfies at least a $1-\\zeta$ fraction of $A$. If $\\varphi$ is unsatisfiable, then $\\mathop{\\mathrm{val}}(\\Phi_\\varphi)<\\nu$. The degree $h$ and the two errors used in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:ugc\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:ugc\">1</a> may be chosen from $\\zeta,\\nu,d,H$ before that theorem supplies the alphabet $R$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-finite-ordering-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $k\\ge2$ and $\\varnothing\\ne\\Pi\\subsetneq S_k$, put $\\rho_\\Pi=|\\Pi|/k!$, and fix $0<\\delta<(1-\\rho_\\Pi)/2$. There are integers $q,H\\ge1$ and positive rationals $\\zeta,\\nu<1$, depending only on $k,\\Pi,\\delta$, with the following property for every fixed integer $R\\ge1$.*\n\n*Let $\\Phi=(A,B,E)$ be a nonempty explicit unweighted simple bipartite Unique Games instance over $[R]$, oriented from $A$ to $B$, whose common left degree $h$ satisfies $h\\ge H$ and $q\\mid h$. There is a deterministic transformation, polynomial in the explicit size of $\\Phi$ for fixed $R$, producing an explicit list of $\\Pi$-ordering constraints on distinct variables within each constraint, with nonnegative rational weights, each encoded by a binary numerator and a positive binary denominator, summing to one. If some labeling strongly satisfies at least $(1-\\zeta)|A|$ left vertices, then $\\mathop{\\mathrm{OPT}}_\\Pi(I)\\ge1-\\delta$. If $\\mathop{\\mathrm{val}}(\\Phi)\\le\\nu$, then $\\mathop{\\mathrm{OPT}}_\\Pi(I)\\le\\rho_\\Pi+\\delta$. The constants $q,H,\\zeta,\\nu$ are independent of both $R$ and $h$.",
        "source_id": "PM-102.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-finite-ordering-transfer",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $k\\ge2$ and $\\varnothing\\ne\\Pi\\subsetneq S_k$, put $\\rho_\\Pi=|\\Pi|/k!$, and fix $0<\\delta<(1-\\rho_\\Pi)/2$. There are integers $q,H\\ge1$ and positive rationals $\\zeta,\\nu<1$, depending only on $k,\\Pi,\\delta$, with the following property for every fixed integer $R\\ge1$.*\n\n*Let $\\Phi=(A,B,E)$ be a nonempty explicit unweighted simple bipartite Unique Games instance over $[R]$, oriented from $A$ to $B$, whose common left degree $h$ satisfies $h\\ge H$ and $q\\mid h$. There is a deterministic transformation, polynomial in the explicit size of $\\Phi$ for fixed $R$, producing an explicit list of $\\Pi$-ordering constraints on distinct variables within each constraint, with nonnegative rational weights, each encoded by a binary numerator and a positive binary denominator, summing to one. If some labeling strongly satisfies at least $(1-\\zeta)|A|$ left vertices, then $\\mathop{\\mathrm{OPT}}_\\Pi(I)\\ge1-\\delta$. If $\\mathop{\\mathrm{val}}(\\Phi)\\le\\nu$, then $\\mathop{\\mathrm{OPT}}_\\Pi(I)\\le\\rho_\\Pi+\\delta$. The constants $q,H,\\zeta,\\nu$ are independent of both $R$ and $h$.",
          "source_id": "PM-102.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix $k\\ge2$ and $\\varnothing\\ne\\Pi\\subsetneq S_k$, put $\\rho_\\Pi=|\\Pi|/k!$, and fix $0<\\delta<(1-\\rho_\\Pi)/2$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-ordering-local-data",
        "exact_quote": "For every integer $q\\ge1$ and rational $\\eta>0$, there are a finite local instance $J=(U,\\mathcal C,\\omega)$, with $|U|=m\\ge2$, and probability laws $\\mu_c$ on $[m]^k$ with the following properties. Every tuple $c$ has $k$ distinct variables; the weights $\\omega_c$ and all atoms of $\\mu_c$ are rational. Every one-position marginal of $\\mu_c$ is uniform on $[m]$, and there is a fixed $0<\\alpha\\le1/2$ such that $\\mu_c(x)>\\alpha$ for every $c,x$. Moreover $$\\operatorname{opt}_q(J)\\le\\rho_\\Pi+\\eta,\\qquad\n v_{\\mathrm{loc}}:=\n \\sum_c\\omega_c\\mathbb E_{X\\sim\\mu_c}\\overline{P_0}(X)\n \\ge1-\\eta.\n\\label{eq:ordering-local-gap}$$ The local laws have common feasible Gram data for the relaxation in (Guruswami et al. 2011, sec. 8.3) [[GuruswamiEtAl2011OrderingCSP](#ref-GuruswamiEtAl2011OrderingCSP)]: there are a unit vector $I$ and vectors $b_{u,j}$, $u\\in U,j\\in[m]$, with $$\\sum_j b_{u,j}=I,\\quad\n \\langle b_{u,i},b_{u,j}\\rangle=0\\ (i\\ne j),\\quad\n \\|b_{u,j}\\|^2=\\frac1m,$$ and, for every two positions $r,s$ of $c$, $$\\langle b_{u_{c,r},i},b_{u_{c,s},j}\\rangle\n =\\Pr_{X\\sim\\mu_c}[X_r=i,\\ X_s=j].\n\\label{eq:ordering-local-moments}$$ All these data are fixed by $k,\\Pi,q,\\eta$.",
        "source_id": "PM-102.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-ordering-local-data",
          "exact_quote": "For every integer $q\\ge1$ and rational $\\eta>0$, there are a finite local instance $J=(U,\\mathcal C,\\omega)$, with $|U|=m\\ge2$, and probability laws $\\mu_c$ on $[m]^k$ with the following properties. Every tuple $c$ has $k$ distinct variables; the weights $\\omega_c$ and all atoms of $\\mu_c$ are rational. Every one-position marginal of $\\mu_c$ is uniform on $[m]$, and there is a fixed $0<\\alpha\\le1/2$ such that $\\mu_c(x)>\\alpha$ for every $c,x$. Moreover $$\\operatorname{opt}_q(J)\\le\\rho_\\Pi+\\eta,\\qquad\n v_{\\mathrm{loc}}:=\n \\sum_c\\omega_c\\mathbb E_{X\\sim\\mu_c}\\overline{P_0}(X)\n \\ge1-\\eta.\n\\label{eq:ordering-local-gap}$$ The local laws have common feasible Gram data for the relaxation in (Guruswami et al. 2011, sec. 8.3) [[GuruswamiEtAl2011OrderingCSP](#ref-GuruswamiEtAl2011OrderingCSP)]: there are a unit vector $I$ and vectors $b_{u,j}$, $u\\in U,j\\in[m]$, with $$\\sum_j b_{u,j}=I,\\quad\n \\langle b_{u,i},b_{u,j}\\rangle=0\\ (i\\ne j),\\quad\n \\|b_{u,j}\\|^2=\\frac1m,$$ and, for every two positions $r,s$ of $c$, $$\\langle b_{u_{c,r},i},b_{u_{c,s},j}\\rangle\n =\\Pr_{X\\sim\\mu_c}[X_r=i,\\ X_s=j].\n\\label{eq:ordering-local-moments}$$ All these data are fixed by $k,\\Pi,q,\\eta$.",
          "source_id": "PM-102.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For every integer $q\\ge1$ and rational $\\eta>0$, there are a finite local instance $J=(U,\\mathcal C,\\omega)$, with $|U|=m\\ge2$, and probability laws $\\mu_c$ on $[m]^k$ with the following properties. Every tuple $c$ has $k$ distinct variables; the weights $\\omega_c$ and all atoms of $\\mu_c$ are rational. Every one-position marginal of $\\mu_c$ is uniform on $[m]$, and there is a fixed $0<\\alpha\\le1/2$ such that $\\mu_c(x)>\\alpha$ for every $c,x$. Moreover $$\\operatorname{opt}_q(J)\\le\\rho_\\Pi+\\eta,\\qquad\n v_{\\mathrm{loc}}:=\n \\sum_c\\omega_c\\mathbb E_{X\\sim\\mu_c}\\overline{P_0}(X)\n \\ge1-\\eta.\n\\label{eq:ordering-local-gap}$$ The local laws have common feasible Gram data for the relaxation in (Guruswami et al. 2011, sec. 8.3) [[GuruswamiEtAl2011OrderingCSP](#ref-GuruswamiEtAl2011OrderingCSP)]: there are a unit vector $I$ and vectors $b_{u,j}$, $u\\in U,j\\in[m]$, with $$\\sum_j b_{u,j}=I,\\quad\n \\langle b_{u,i},b_{u,j}\\rangle=0\\ (i\\ne j),\\quad\n \\|b_{u,j}\\|^2=\\frac1m,$$ and, for every two positions $r,s$ of $c$, $$\\langle b_{u_{c,r},i},b_{u_{c,s},j}\\rangle\n =\\Pr_{X\\sim\\mu_c}[X_r=i,\\ X_s=j].\n\\label{eq:ordering-local-moments}$$ All these data are fixed by $k,\\Pi,q,\\eta$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-ordering-collision",
        "exact_quote": "For every $0<\\kappa<1/2$, there is $\\mu_0(\\kappa)>0$. For each $m\\ge2$, there is a nonnegative function $r_c^{m,\\kappa}(t)\\to0$ as $t\\downarrow0$ with the following property, uniformly over all $R\\ge1$. Let $F,G:[m]^R\\to[0,1]$ have the same mean $0<\\mu\\le\\mu_0(\\kappa)$ and satisfy $$\\operatorname{Inf}_\\ell(T_{1-\\kappa}F)\\le t,\\qquad\n \\operatorname{Inf}_\\ell(T_{1-\\kappa}G)\\le t\n \\quad(\\ell\\in[R]).$$ For every coupling $(Z,Z')$ whose two marginals are uniform on $[m]^R$, $$\\mathbb E\\bigl[\n   (T_{1-2\\kappa}F)(Z)(T_{1-2\\kappa}G)(Z')\\bigr]\n \\le \\mu^{1+\\kappa/2}+r_c^{m,\\kappa}(t).\n\\label{eq:ordering-collision}$$ The function $r_c^{m,\\kappa}$ is independent of $R,\\mu,F,G$ and the coupling.",
        "source_id": "PM-102.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-ordering-collision",
          "exact_quote": "For every $0<\\kappa<1/2$, there is $\\mu_0(\\kappa)>0$. For each $m\\ge2$, there is a nonnegative function $r_c^{m,\\kappa}(t)\\to0$ as $t\\downarrow0$ with the following property, uniformly over all $R\\ge1$. Let $F,G:[m]^R\\to[0,1]$ have the same mean $0<\\mu\\le\\mu_0(\\kappa)$ and satisfy $$\\operatorname{Inf}_\\ell(T_{1-\\kappa}F)\\le t,\\qquad\n \\operatorname{Inf}_\\ell(T_{1-\\kappa}G)\\le t\n \\quad(\\ell\\in[R]).$$ For every coupling $(Z,Z')$ whose two marginals are uniform on $[m]^R$, $$\\mathbb E\\bigl[\n   (T_{1-2\\kappa}F)(Z)(T_{1-2\\kappa}G)(Z')\\bigr]\n \\le \\mu^{1+\\kappa/2}+r_c^{m,\\kappa}(t).\n\\label{eq:ordering-collision}$$ The function $r_c^{m,\\kappa}$ is independent of $R,\\mu,F,G$ and the coupling.",
          "source_id": "PM-102.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For every $0<\\kappa<1/2$, there is $\\mu_0(\\kappa)>0$. For each $m\\ge2$, there is a nonnegative function $r_c^{m,\\kappa}(t)\\to0$ as $t\\downarrow0$ with the following property, uniformly over all $R\\ge1$. Let $F,G:[m]^R\\to[0,1]$ have the same mean $0<\\mu\\le\\mu_0(\\kappa)$ and satisfy $$\\operatorname{Inf}_\\ell(T_{1-\\kappa}F)\\le t,\\qquad\n \\operatorname{Inf}_\\ell(T_{1-\\kappa}G)\\le t\n \\quad(\\ell\\in[R]).$$ For every coupling $(Z,Z')$ whose two marginals are uniform on $[m]^R$, $$\\mathbb E\\bigl[\n   (T_{1-2\\kappa}F)(Z)(T_{1-2\\kappa}G)(Z')\\bigr]\n \\le \\mu^{1+\\kappa/2}+r_c^{m,\\kappa}(t).\n\\label{eq:ordering-collision}$$ The function $r_c^{m,\\kappa}$ is independent of $R,\\mu,F,G$ and the coupling.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Unique Games instance** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A *Unique Games instance* has a finite vertex set, a finite alphabet $K$, and a nonempty list of oriented constraints $e=(u_e,v_e,\pi_e)$, where $\pi_e$ is a permutation of $K$.
- **Labeling value and explicit unweighted representation** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A labeling $a$ satisfies $e$ when $a(v_e)=\pi_e(a(u_e))$. Its value is the fraction of satisfied constraints, and $\mathop{\mathrm{val}}(G)$ is the maximum value of a labeling. The instance is *explicit and unweighted* when the entire vertex and constraint lists are written out, including the table of each permutation.
- **Bipartite projection game** ([source](#pm-h-8)): A bipartite *projection game* has a question for each vertex on either side, finite answer sets, and a map from left answers to right answers on each constraint.
- **Parallel power** ([source](#pm-h-8)): The parallel power $G^{\otimes t}$ samples $t$ independent constraint occurrences, sends the ordered question tuples, and accepts if every coordinate accepts.
- **Restriction rank** ([source](#pm-h-9)): A subspace $S\le E^*$ restricts to its image $S\|_K\le K^*$ on a subspace $K\le E$; its restriction rank is $\dim(S\|_K)$.
- **Normalized binary Fourier coefficients** ([source](#pm-h-9)): For a real function $f$ on a binary space $E$, use normalized Fourier coefficients $$\widehat f(g)=\mathbb E_{x\in E}f(x)(-1)^{g(x)},\qquad f(x)=\sum_{g\in E^*}\widehat f(g)(-1)^{g(x)},\qquad \sum_g\|\widehat f(g)\|^2=\mathbb E_x\|f(x)\|^2.$$
- **Total variation distance** ([source](#pm-h-10)): For finite probability laws, $$\mathop{\mathrm{TV}}(P,Q)=\frac12\sum_x\|P(x)-Q(x)\|.$$
- **Generic character subspace** ([source](#stmt-auto-definition-1)): A binary subspace $S\le F^3$ of dimension $r\ge1$ is *generic* if no two distinct nonzero elements of $S$ are proportional over $F$, and if, for every binary linear map $g:S\to F^3$, at most $3r$ nonzero $z\in S$ satisfy $$\label{eq:latent-alignment} z\cdot g(z)=\sqrt{z_1z_2z_3}.$$
- **Linear lift** ([source](#pm-h-17)): For $S\le B^*$, a *lift* on $P_t$ means a binary linear map $z\mapsto\psi_z$ from $S$ to $P_t^*$ satisfying $$\label{eq:latent-lift-definition} \psi_z\|_{R_t}=z\circ\lambda_t.$$
- **Full lift** ([source](#pm-h-17)): A lift is *full* when its domain is $B^*$.
- **Weighted parity instance** ([source](#pm-h-20)): A *weighted parity instance* consists of a finite variable set $\mathcal X$, a finite nonempty set $\mathcal E$ of equation occurrences, and positive rational weights $\omega_e$ summing to one.
- **Affine table** ([source](#pm-h-22)): A *table* on a question $Q$ is an affine map from its answer space to $\mathcal V$, or equivalently its unique linear extension $P\in\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(L_Q,\mathcal V)$.
- **Exact table key** ([source](#eq:reduced-table-intercept)): We define the *key* of $(Q,P)$ to be its support together with this entire reduced table.
- **Restored answer folding** ([source](#eq:table-folding)): Adding a constant changes only the removed component, so $$F_Q(P+h e_0^\top)=F_Q(P)+h \qquad(h\in K). \label{eq:table-folding}$$
- **Row and column slice** ([source](#eq:shortcode-slice)): A *row and column slice* is a nonempty set specified by equations $$\mathcal S= \{M:d_i^\top M=s_i^\top\ (1\le i\le n_r),\quad Mq_j=t_j\ (1\le j\le n_c)\}, \label{eq:shortcode-slice}$$
- **Projected advice experiment** ([source](#stmt-def-projected-advice)): Sample $U,O,\pi$, then an independent uniform map $A:K\to\mathbb F_2^{r_{\rm s}}$ and independent uniform maps $$T_O:L_O\to W,\qquad M_O:L_O\to K.$$ Put $S_O=AM_O$ and send the following inputs to the two provers: $$\begin{aligned} &\text{$U$-prover:}&& (U,A,T_O\bar\pi,S_O\bar\pi),\\ &\text{$O$-prover:}&& (O,A,T_O,S_O). \end{aligned} \label{eq:projected-advice}$$ The map $M_O$ beyond the stated advice is hidden. Each prover returns an actual answer, that is, a vector of first bit one in its homogeneous answer space. They succeed when their answers $a_U,a_O$ satisfy $\bar\pi a_U=a_O$.
- **Row fiber** ([source](#pm-h-28)): For fixed $U,T,A$ and a map $S_0:L_U\to\mathbb F_2^{r_{\rm s}}$, the set $\{M:AM=S_0\}$ is its *row fiber*.
- **Good row advice** ([source](#pm-h-28)): We call the row advice $(U,T,A,S_0)$ *good* if there exist at most $r_{\rm s}$ column specifications and a target $Mz+u$ such that the simultaneous solution set of the column specifications and $AM=S_0$ is nonempty and $F_U(M,T)=Mz+u$ with probability at least $\alpha/2$ under the uniform measure on this set.
- **Clean coordinate** ([source](#eq:clean-joint-law)): Call coordinate $i$ *clean* if it is projected to a singleton and its one advice slope $d_i\in E_A$ is zero.
- **Strongly satisfied left vertex** ([source](#pm-h-45)): A left vertex is *strongly satisfied* by a labeling when every edge incident to it is satisfied.

<!-- PM:END pm.objects.v2 -->

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-unique-games-completeness | Final game value on a satisfiable formula | ≥ | $1-\varepsilon$ | The errors determine the fixed alphabet and reduction polynomial. |
| q-unique-games-soundness | Final game value on an unsatisfiable formula | ≤ | $\delta$ | Soundness is prescribed before the alphabet is chosen. |
| q-latent-output-change | Latent nonlinear output change probability | ≤ | $p_*$ | The rank threshold precedes the output dimension. |
| q-latent-linear-detection | High-restriction-rank noise detection probability | ≥ | $\frac18$ | The threshold depends only on the prescribed latent error. |
| q-block-line-density | Reciprocal number of field lines | = | $\frac{1}{q^2+q+1}$ | The field is fixed after the initial rank budget. |
| q-recursive-change-probability | Recursive nonlinear output change probability | = | $(1-q^{-3})(1-\theta)^t$ | The field and block law remain fixed as height varies. |
| q-full-lift-detection | Full-lift detection probability at the selected height | ≥ | $\frac14$ | The height is selected from the harmonic rank budget. |
| q-distinct-parity-optimum-inflation | Optimum after distinct-position preprocessing | ≤ | $\frac{1+\mathop{\mathrm{OPT}}(\mathcal E)}2+\frac3{100}$ | The clone construction is fixed independently of input size. |
| q-matrix-game-completeness | Outer matrix-game value | ≥ | $1-k\xi-\frac{p_*}{2}$ | Latent data precede tuple length, which precedes the parity error. |
| q-good-row-advice-mass | Probability of good row advice | ≥ | $g_0$ | The positive lower bound depends only on fixed latent and shortcode parameters. |
| q-row-answer-set-size | Selected actual-answer set cardinality | ≤ | $2^{r_{\rm s}}$ | The shortcode column budget bounds the answer list independently of tuple length. |
| q-conditional-fourier-decoding | Conditional full-answer agreement probability | ≥ | $2^{-\ell r_{\rm s}}(\alpha/8)^2\,2^{-r_{\rm s}}$ | All factors depend only on the fixed advice and shortcode parameters. |
| q-projection-repetition-value | Classical value of the independent repeated projection game | ≤ | $(1-g^2/16)^n$ | The estimate is independent of question and answer alphabet sizes. |
| q-clean-advice-agreement | Advice-strategy full agreement probability | ≤ | $\left(1-\frac{\beta\,2^{-\dim W-r_{\rm s}}}{3600}\right)^k$ | Tuple length grows along cubes with the specified sparse projection rate. |
| q-rounded-subdivision-error | Deviation from the ideal subdivision value transformation | ≤ | $\frac{\tau}{4}$ | Fixed rounding error gives polynomial construction time in the explicit input encoding. |
| q-ordering-random-baseline | Random-order satisfaction baseline | = | $\|\Pi\|/k!$ | The arity and allowed relative permutations are fixed. |
| q-ordering-collision-bound | Coupled smoothed-function product expectation | ≤ | $\mu^{1+\kappa/2}+r_c^{m,\kappa}(t)$ | The error is uniform in product dimension, the common mean, and the coupling. |
| q-simplex-local-payoff | Expected noisy multilinear ordering payoff | ≤ | $\operatorname{opt}_q(J)+r_s(kqt)$ | The error depends on the fixed local data and noise, independently of product dimension. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-quadratic-update-algebra** [claim](#stmt-lem-latent-block) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-latent-block): Expand the quadratic polar expression, use trace annihilator dimensions, and inspect coordinate lines. -> Binary linear update spaces, polar cancellation, annihilators, and spanning.
- **step-block-response-law** [claim](#stmt-lem-latent-block-noise) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-latent-block-noise): Compute the response for a uniform update, then condition away the zero update. -> The exact block change probability.
- **step-character-kernel** [claim](#stmt-lem-latent-block-kernel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-latent-block-kernel): Annihilate the vertical hyperplane and apply nondegeneracy of the trace pairing to the remaining field-line vectors. -> The exact field-line and alignment criterion for a kernel element.
- **step-genericity-abundance** [claim](#stmt-lem-latent-generic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-latent-generic): Parametrize random subspaces, rule out symbolic column membership by monomial independence, and specialize a nonzero minor using a polynomial zero bound. -> A field dimension where nongeneric subspaces are rare at all required ranks.
- **step-recursive-output-stability** [claim](#stmt-lem-latent-noise) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-latent-noise): Use shift equivariance to establish conditional uniformity of the initial aggregate, then apply the independent nonzero block response law. -> The exact geometric formula for output change probability.
- **step-lift-factorization** [claim](#stmt-lem-latent-lift-factorization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-latent-lift-factorization): Factor characters through child output translations and the surjective aggregate map. -> A common extension map governing every child character restriction.
- **step-harmonic-rank-survival** [claim](#stmt-prop-latent-full-lift-detection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-latent-full-lift-detection): Track harmonic rank loss along a random branch, average fresh isomorphism orientations, and detect the independent leaf symbol on survival. -> A uniform detection lower bound for every full lift.
- **step-latent-quotient-and-enlargement** [claim](#stmt-lem-latent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-latent-full-lift-detection-2): Quotient zero-valued shifts, combine copies indexed by injections into the larger output space, and use a restriction-surjectivity union bound. -> The equivariant stable nonlinear map and high-rank linear detection gadget.
- **step-import-parity-gap** [claim](#stmt-thm-parity) / [proof](#pm-h-20): Enumerate the fixed parity verifier choices with their rational probabilities. -> A deterministic weighted parity gap reduction.
- **step-distinct-position-preprocessing** [claim](#stmt-lem-distinct-parity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-distinct-parity): Clone variables, condition repeated positions to use distinct clones, and compare arbitrary clone labels with majority rounding. -> Distinct variable IDs within every occurrence with controlled optimum inflation.
- **step-folded-matrix-completeness** [claim](#stmt-lem-outer-completeness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-outer-completeness): Evaluate reduced keys on support answers induced by a global assignment, then bound unsatisfied tuples and latent changes. -> Adjustable near-perfect matrix-game completeness.
- **step-shortcode-inverse** [claim](#stmt-thm-shortcode) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-shortcode): Select a retaining equality fiber, transfer it through the matrix chart to the Grassmann graph, and translate an expansion interval into row and column equations. -> Positive affine-evaluation agreement on a bounded-specification nonempty slice.
- **step-noise-spectral-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-matrix-spectral) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-matrix-spectral): Diagonalize rank-one noise by matrix characters and compare latent and uniform-alphabet multipliers using restriction rank. -> A positive fraction of fibers with ordinary shortcode acceptance.
- **step-good-row-advice** [claim](#stmt-lem-good-row-advice) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-good-row-advice): Erase the union of useful fibers, choose replacements by concentration, contradict the inverse theorem, and use folding to obtain actual answers in the target coset. -> A positive mass of good row advice and deterministic short nonempty answer sets.
- **step-padded-table-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-projected-tv) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-projected-tv): Compute a block likelihood-ratio divergence, multiply independent block ratios, and retain intercept uniformity under projection corrections. -> Uniform total variation convergence of full padded tables to unrestricted tables.
- **step-witness-transfer** [claim](#stmt-lem-projected-witness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-projected-witness): Transfer a positive difference of event probabilities between full table laws and disintegrate over joint visible advice. -> Positive mass of nonempty projected slices retaining target agreement.
- **step-fourier-coset-decoding** [claim](#stmt-lem-fourier-coset-decoding) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-fourier-coset-decoding): Expand correlation on the transferred affine slice, bound Fourier coset mass by Cauchy–Schwarz, and contract a sampled frequency. -> A positive conditional lower bound on actual-answer agreement.
- **step-matrix-decoding-lower-bound** [claim](#stmt-prop-matrix-decoding) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-fourier-coset-decoding-2): Multiply transferred-data mass by conditional decoder agreement, then average private random tapes. -> Local strategies with fixed positive full-answer agreement.
- **step-import-classical-repetition** [claim](#stmt-thm-repetition) / [proof](#pm-h-32): Apply the cited alphabet-independent projection-game repetition theorem with its weighted occurrence and classical product conventions. -> An exponential upper bound for independent parallel repetition.
- **step-clean-coordinate-upper-bound** [claim](#stmt-lem-clean) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-clean): Condition on clean positions and outside data, reconstruct each local input separately, apply repetition to clean questions, and average their binomial count. -> An agreement upper bound tending to zero with tuple length.
- **step-matrix-gap-contradiction** [claim](#stmt-prop-matrix-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-matrix-gap): Compare the fixed decoded agreement lower bound with the vanishing clean-coordinate upper bound. -> A matrix game with adjustable completeness and a fixed NO gap.
- **step-round-and-subdivide** [claim](#stmt-lem-unweighted-subdivision) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-unweighted-subdivision): Round rational weights by fractional remainders and replace each occurrence by a path with fresh interior vertices. -> An unweighted simple bipartite game with the stated value transformation.
- **step-final-unique-games-reduction** [claim](#stmt-thm-ugc) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-unweighted-subdivision-2): Fix the final repetition exponent before the alphabet, choose latent and shortcode data before tuple length and parity error, enumerate the matrix game, round, subdivide, and take the product. -> The explicit deterministic polynomial reduction with simple bipartite translation constraints.
- **step-simple-strong-left-normalization** [claim](#stmt-lem-simple-strong-left) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-simple-strong-left): Normalize left weights, round local occurrences, introduce right tags, form occurrence sequences, and retain sequences with distinct tags. -> Simple common-left-degree games with strong-left completeness and low edge value.
- **step-fixed-csp-consequence** [claim](#stmt-cor-raghavendra-consequence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-raghavendra-consequence): Apply the cited fixed-instance relaxation-gap theorem through the strong-left interface, then rationalize the fixed local law and enumerate neighbor queries. -> The stated rational weighted CSP gap.
- **step-rational-ordering-local-data** [claim](#stmt-lem-ordering-local-data) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ordering-local-data): Take finite copies of the cited increasing-tuple construction, smooth a complete order by cyclic shifts and independent symbols, and form indicator Gram vectors. -> Rational full-support laws with uniform marginals, a local gap, and common feasible moments.
- **step-ordering-collision-control** [claim](#stmt-lem-ordering-collision) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ordering-collision): Compare smoothed norms with noise stability, apply the cited low-influence estimate and Gaussian small-mean bound, and use Cauchy–Schwarz for arbitrary couplings. -> A dimension-uniform collision bound.
- **step-simplex-invariance-estimate** [claim](#stmt-lem-ordering-rounding) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ordering-rounding): Factor independent noise through multilinearity, check vector influence and tail bounds, compare common integral and Gaussian ensembles, and project to the simplex. -> A local payoff bound by the coarse optimum plus dimension-uniform error.
- **step-finite-ordering-composition** [claim](#stmt-prop-finite-ordering-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ordering-rounding-2): Compose independent neighbor queries, coarsen an arbitrary order into equal blocks, decode influential coordinates, condition on distinct neighbors, and enumerate rational outcomes. -> Explicit distinct-variable ordering constraints with the required completeness and soundness gap.
- **step-ordering-hardness-threshold** [claim](#stmt-cor-ordering-csp) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-ordering-csp): Compose the finite ordering transfer with strong-left hardness and choose a strict error budget below the requested improvement over random ordering. -> Optimal strict approximation hardness for the finite weighted ordering model.
- **step-cut-deletion-clustering-consequences** [claim](#stmt-cor-cut-clustering) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-cut-clustering): Supply the ordinary edge-value premise to the cited cut reductions, retain the search qualification, and transfer Multicut hardness to arbitrary weighted correlation clustering. -> Fixed-factor hardness for the stated cut, deletion, and clustering models.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-rounded-occurrence-budget**: Largest-remainder rounding produces a nonnegative integer multiplicity for each occurrence, with the prescribed total multiplicity and controlled weight error.
- **inv-simple-bipartite-translations**: Subdivision produces an unweighted simple bipartite game and preserves binary translation constraints, including when the original instance contains loops or repeated occurrences.
- **inv-subdivision-value-identity**: For the rounded multigraph, subdivision converts each unsatisfied occurrence into exactly one unsatisfied edge in an optimal extension of the original labels.
- **inv-rounded-subdivision-error**: The optimum of the final subdivided game obeys the stated rounding-and-subdivision error bound relative to the original weighted game.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-finite-tests-certify-main-theorem**: Treat successful finite subdivision checks as certification of the full Unique Games reduction. -> These checks cover a finite translation-game construction; they do not establish latent detection, matrix decoding, or the asymptotic soundness contradiction.
- **anti-efficient-decoding-assumption**: Implement the analytic Fourier decoders as an efficient solver justified by the reduction's polynomial running time. -> The soundness strategies may require inefficient witness selection and Fourier sampling.
- **anti-reused-path-interiors**: Share subdivision vertices between repeated occurrences to reduce the output size. -> The independent optimization of path interiors no longer follows, and repeated endpoint pairs may destroy simplicity.
- **anti-uniform-parameter-runtime**: Read the polynomial reduction guarantee as a uniform practical running-time bound while the prescribed errors vary. -> The alphabet and polynomial constants depend on the errors; the auxiliary searches occur before the input formula varies.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The rounding-and-subdivision lemma gives a deterministic finite construction. This harness checks its binary translation specialization with exact rational arithmetic and exhaustive labeling enumeration. It does not certify the full Unique Games theorem.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON n, edges [u,v,offset,weight_numerator,weight_denominator], tau [numerator,denominator].
# PRECONDITIONS: Nonempty positive rational weights sum to one; valid endpoints; binary offsets; positive tau.
# OUTPUTS: JSON explicit simple bipartite binary translation game with unweighted edges.
# TIME: Polynomial in the listed input and emitted graph size, using exact rational arithmetic.
# SPACE: Linear in the listed input and emitted graph size, apart from exact arithmetic storage.
# ARITHMETIC: Arbitrary precision integers and exact rational numbers.
# FAILURE: Invalid input raises an exception and produces no game certificate.
import json
import sys
from fractions import Fraction

def main():
    data = json.load(sys.stdin)
    n = data['n']
    rows = data['edges']
    assert type(n) is int and n >= 1 and rows
    tau = Fraction(*data['tau'])
    assert tau > 0
    weights = []
    for row in rows:
        assert len(row) == 5 and all(type(x) is int for x in row)
        u, v, c, a, b = row
        assert 0 <= u < n and 0 <= v < n and c in (0, 1)
        assert a > 0 and b > 0
        weights.append(Fraction(a, b))
    assert sum(weights) == 1
    h = len(rows)
    ratio = Fraction(h, 1) / tau
    Q = (ratio.numerator + ratio.denominator - 1) // ratio.denominator
    quotas = [Q * w for w in weights]
    counts = [q.numerator // q.denominator for q in quotas]
    order = sorted(range(h), key=lambda i: (-(quotas[i] - counts[i]), i))
    remaining = Q - sum(counts)
    for i in order[:remaining]:
        counts[i] += 1
    left = list(range(n))
    right = []
    edges = []
    next_vertex = n
    for row, copies in zip(rows, counts):
        u, v, c, _, _ = row
        for _ in range(copies):
            p, q, r = next_vertex, next_vertex + 1, next_vertex + 2
            next_vertex += 3
            left.append(q)
            right.extend([p, r])
            edges.append([u, p, 0])
            edges.append([q, p, 0])
            edges.append([q, r, 0])
            edges.append([v, r, c])
    json.dump({'n': next_vertex, 'left': left, 'right': right, 'edges': edges}, sys.stdout, sort_keys=True)

if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()
```

    "oracle_code": "import json\nimport sys\nfrom fractions import Fraction\nfrom itertools import combinations, product\nfrom collections import Counter\n\ndef require(condition):\n    if not condition:\n        raise ValueError('certificate rejected')\n\ndef optimum(n, rows, weights):\n    best = Fraction(-1)\n    for labels in product((0, 1), repeat=n):\n        score = sum((w for (u, v, c), w in zip(rows, weights)\n                     if (labels[u] ^ labels[v]) == c), Fraction(0))\n        best = max(best, score)\n    return best\n\ndef check(data, graph, expected):\n    require(expected == 'valid')\n    n = data['n']\n    source = data['edges']\n    tau = Fraction(*data['tau'])\n    h = len(source)\n    weights = [Fraction(row[3], row[4]) for row in source]\n    ratio = Fraction(h, 1) / tau\n    Q = -(-ratio.numerator // ratio.denominator)\n    quotas = [Q * w for w in weights]\n    floors = [q.numerator // q.denominator for q in quotas]\n    residual = Q - sum(floors)\n    fractions = [q - f for q, f in zip(quotas, floors)]\n    # Independently characterize largest-remainder rounding by finite subset optimization.\n    choices = list(combinations(range(h), residual))\n    selected = min(choices, key=lambda subset: (-sum((fractions[i] for i in subset), Fraction(0)), subset))\n    counts = [floors[i] + int(i in selected) for i in range(h)]\n    required_paths = Counter()\n    for row, count in zip(source, counts):\n        u, v, c = row[:3]\n        required_paths[(min(u, v), max(u, v), c)] += count\n    required_paths = +required_paths\n    require(type(graph) is dict and set(graph) == {'n', 'left', 'right', 'edges'})\n    N = graph['n']\n    require(type(N) is int and N == n + 3 * Q)\n    left_list, right_list = graph['left'], graph['right']\n    require(all(type(v) is int for v in left_list + right_list))\n    left, right = set(left_list), set(right_list)\n    require(len(left) == len(left_list) and len(right) == len(right_list))\n    require(not (left & right) and left | right == set(range(N)))\n    require(set(range(n)) <= left and len(left) == n + Q and len(right) == 2 * Q)\n    listed = graph['edges']\n    require(len(listed) == 4 * Q)\n    adjacency = [[] for _ in range(N)]\n    pairs = set()\n    for edge in listed:\n        require(type(edge) is list and len(edge) == 3)\n        require(all(type(x) is int for x in edge))\n        u, v, c = edge\n        require(u in left and v in right and c in (0, 1))\n        require((u, v) not in pairs)\n        pairs.add((u, v))\n        adjacency[u].append((v, c))\n        adjacency[v].append((u, c))\n    # Recover paths from topology instead of reconstructing the candidate's vertex numbering.\n    actual_paths = Counter()\n    consumed_right = set()\n    for q in left - set(range(n)):\n        require(len(adjacency[q]) == 2)\n        endpoints = []\n        total_offset = 0\n        for r, offset in adjacency[q]:\n            require(r in right and r not in consumed_right)\n            consumed_right.add(r)\n            require(len(adjacency[r]) == 2)\n            others = [(v, c) for v, c in adjacency[r] if v != q]\n            require(len(others) == 1)\n            v, c = others[0]\n            require(0 <= v < n)\n            endpoints.append(v)\n            total_offset ^= offset ^ c\n        u, v = endpoints\n        actual_paths[(min(u, v), max(u, v), total_offset)] += 1\n    require(consumed_right == right and actual_paths == required_paths)\n    original_rows = [tuple(row[:3]) for row in source]\n    original_value = optimum(n, original_rows, weights)\n    rounded_value = optimum(n, original_rows, [Fraction(c, Q) for c in counts])\n    final_value = optimum(N, [tuple(e) for e in listed], [Fraction(1, 4 * Q)] * (4 * Q))\n    require(abs(rounded_value - original_value) <= tau)\n    require(final_value == 1 - (1 - rounded_value) / 4)\n    require(abs(final_value - (1 - (1 - original_value) / 4)) <= tau / 4)\n\ntry:\n    with open(sys.argv[1], encoding='utf-8') as stream:\n        data = json.load(stream)\n    with open(sys.argv[2], encoding='utf-8') as stream:\n        graph = json.load(stream)\n    check(data, graph, sys.argv[3])\nexcept Exception:\n    sys.exit(1)\nsys.exit(0)\n",
    "output_contract": "One JSON object with n output vertices, a partition left/right, and an unweighted list edges of [left_vertex,right_vertex,offset]. Each rounded occurrence is represented by a fresh path of four edges. The independent checker validates the rounded occurrence multiset, graph structure, exact subdivision value identity, and final error bound.",
    "reason": "The rounding-and-subdivision lemma gives a deterministic finite construction. This harness checks its binary translation specialization with exact rational arithmetic and exhaustive labeling enumeration. It does not certify the full Unique Games theorem.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON n, edges [u,v,offset,weight_numerator,weight_denominator], tau [numerator,denominator].\n# PRECONDITIONS: Nonempty positive rational weights sum to one; valid endpoints; binary offsets; positive tau.\n# OUTPUTS: JSON explicit simple bipartite binary translation game with unweighted edges.\n# TIME: Polynomial in the listed input and emitted graph size, using exact rational arithmetic.\n# SPACE: Linear in the listed input and emitted graph size, apart from exact arithmetic storage.\n# ARITHMETIC: Arbitrary precision integers and exact rational numbers.\n# FAILURE: Invalid input raises an exception and produces no game certificate.\nimport json\nimport sys\nfrom fractions import Fraction\n\ndef main():\n    data = json.load(sys.stdin)\n    n = data['n']\n    rows = data['edges']\n    assert type(n) is int and n >= 1 and rows\n    tau = Fraction(*data['tau'])\n    assert tau > 0\n    weights = []\n    for row in rows:\n        assert len(row) == 5 and all(type(x) is int for x in row)\n        u, v, c, a, b = row\n        assert 0 <= u < n and 0 <= v < n and c in (0, 1)\n        assert a > 0 and b > 0\n        weights.append(Fraction(a, b))\n    assert sum(weights) == 1\n    h = len(rows)\n    ratio = Fraction(h, 1) / tau\n    Q = (ratio.numerator + ratio.denominator - 1) // ratio.denominator\n    quotas = [Q * w for w in weights]\n    counts = [q.numerator // q.denominator for q in quotas]\n    order = sorted(range(h), key=lambda i: (-(quotas[i] - counts[i]), i))\n    remaining = Q - sum(counts)\n    for i in order[:remaining]:\n        counts[i] += 1\n    left = list(range(n))\n    right = []\n    edges = []\n    next_vertex = n\n    for row, copies in zip(rows, counts):\n        u, v, c, _, _ = row\n        for _ in range(copies):\n            p, q, r = next_vertex, next_vertex + 1, next_vertex + 2\n            next_vertex += 3\n            left.append(q)\n            right.extend([p, r])\n            edges.append([u, p, 0])\n            edges.append([q, p, 0])\n            edges.append([q, r, 0])\n            edges.append([v, r, c])\n    json.dump({'n': next_vertex, 'left': left, 'right': right, 'edges': edges}, sys.stdout, sort_keys=True)\n\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    main()\n",

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The explicit reductions specify mathematical instance transformations, but do not supply a software architecture or a general program optimisation method. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The games are constraint systems whose value measures agreement between local answer strategies. The construction does not define interactive game mechanics or a physical simulation. |
| tools | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | The deterministic random-order guarantee supplies a concrete procedure for an ordering-constraint tool: extend a prefix by a variable whose exact conditional expected payoff is at least the average available choice. |
| algorithms | True | strong | implementable | inferred | Largest-remainder rounding followed by subdivision gives a finite deterministic transformation from a rationally weighted permutation game to an unweighted simple bipartite game, with a stated comparison of their optimal values. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | The exact table key is a concrete canonical representation of an affine table after removing irrelevant coordinates. It preserves retained positions, tags, IDs, slopes, and the corrected intercept, allowing a projected table and its pullback to share a key. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Binary matrix operations describe a finite reduction and its soundness analysis. The paper gives no accelerator layout, execution schedule, or hardware resource contract. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The stated guarantee separates satisfiable and unsatisfiable formulas through a deterministic reduction. It does not construct a cryptographic primitive or establish a security experiment. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Correlation clustering is specified as a weighted disagreement objective, and the consequence concerns worst-case approximation hardness. No statistical model, training rule, or prediction guarantee is constructed. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The finite ordering transfer constructs rationally weighted ordering instances from a simple strong-left Unique Games instance. It provides a precise reduction contract for studying approximation barriers in ordering optimisation. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The local provers receive prescribed questions and advice, and the analysis concerns their answer agreement. It does not construct communication, replication, or fault-recovery mechanisms. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No additional engineering construction outside the listed domains is established by the supplied corpus. Separately mentioned companion results do not supply an additional implementation contract here. |
