\label{eq:ordering-influence-average}$$ Each term is at most one. An average greater than $t$ forces more than a $t/2$ fraction of the terms to exceed $t/2$. The restriction of a global-rank interval to one right code is an interval of its relative order, so these neighbors satisfy $\pi_{a\to b}(\ell_a)\in\mathcal L_b$.

Give each such $a$ label $\ell_a$, and label every other left vertex arbitrarily. Independently at each right vertex with $\mathcal L_b\ne\varnothing$, choose one uniform label from $\mathcal L_b$; use any label when the set is empty. By <a href="#eq:ordering-right-candidates" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ordering-right-candidates">[eq:ordering-right-candidates]</a>, the expected satisfied fraction among the edges at each nonpseudorandom $a$ is greater than $$\frac t2\,\frac{\kappa t^3}{3200}
 =\frac{\kappa t^4}{6400}.$$ One random right labeling is used for all left vertices. Linearity of expectation and the common left degree identify the resulting average with the ordinary uniform edge fraction.

<a id="eq:ordering-iid-soundness"></a>
If $V(O)>S_{\mathrm{loc}}+\chi$, more than a $\chi$ fraction of left vertices are nonpseudorandom: if that fraction is $g$, the bound $0\le V_a(O)\le1$ and <a href="#eq:ordering-pseudorandom-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ordering-pseudorandom-bound">[eq:ordering-pseudorandom-bound]</a> give $V(O)\le(1-g)S_{\mathrm{loc}}+g\le S_{\mathrm{loc}}+g$. The preceding decoder would then give a labeling of edge value greater than $\chi\kappa t^4/6400$. Consequently $$\mathop{\mathrm{val}}(\Phi)\le\nu<\frac{\chi\kappa t^4}{6400}
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 V(O)\le S_{\mathrm{loc}}+\chi\quad\text{for every injective }O.
\label{eq:ordering-iid-soundness}$$

It remains to turn the independent experiment into strict ordering constraints. Let $D$ be the event that $b_1,\ldots,b_k$ are distinct. For $h\ge k$, <a id="eq:ordering-distinct-probability"></a>$$p_D:=\Pr(D)=\frac{(h)_k}{h^k},\qquad
 \delta_h:=1-p_D\le\frac{\binom{k}{2}}h.
\label{eq:ordering-distinct-probability}$$ The probability is the same at every $a$, and $D$ is independent of the local experiment. Conditional on $D$, $a$ remains uniform and its ordered distinct neighbor tuple is uniform among its $(h)_k$ possibilities. All output IDs are then distinct because their first coordinates $b_i$ differ. Let $I_0$ denote this finite weighted distribution of strict $P_0$ constraints, and write $\mathop{\mathrm{OPT}}_{P_0}(I_0)$ for its maximum strict payoff.

<a id="eq:ordering-conditioning"></a>
<a id="eq:ordering-conditioned-gap"></a>
Let $V_D(f)$ denote the expected tie-extended payoff after this conditioning, and use $V_{\neg D}(f)$ for conditioning on its complement. For any rank map $f$, coarse or injective, $$V(f)=p_DV_D(f)+(1-p_D)V_{\neg D}(f),
 \qquad |V_D(f)-V(f)|\le\delta_h,
\label{eq:ordering-conditioning}$$ because all payoffs lie in $[0,1]$. Apply this first to the coarse map in <a href="#eq:ordering-iid-completeness" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ordering-iid-completeness">[eq:ordering-iid-completeness]</a>. Within each finite fiber of that map, choose one uniform random permutation of the distinct IDs, independently between fibers, and concatenate the fibers in rank order. On every conditioned tuple, restriction of these permutations to its IDs is uniform within each tied rank class. Thus the expected strict $P_0$-payoff of this one random global order equals $V_D(f)$. Some global order attains at least that value. For soundness, an injective $O$ has distinct ranks on every conditioned tuple, so its tie-extended payoff there is its strict $P_0$-payoff. Equations <a href="#eq:ordering-iid-completeness" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ordering-iid-completeness">[eq:ordering-iid-completeness]</a>, <a href="#eq:ordering-iid-soundness" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ordering-iid-soundness">[eq:ordering-iid-soundness]</a>, and <a href="#eq:ordering-conditioning" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ordering-conditioning">[eq:ordering-conditioning]</a> therefore give the strict bounds $$\begin{aligned}
 \mathrm{YES}:\quad&
 \mathop{\mathrm{OPT}}_{P_0}(I_0)\ge(1-\zeta)(1-2\kappa)^k v_{\mathrm{loc}}-\delta_h,\\
 \mathrm{NO}:\quad&
 \mathop{\mathrm{OPT}}_{P_0}(I_0)\le S_{\mathrm{loc}}+\chi+\delta_h
 \quad\text{if }\nu<\chi\kappa t^4/6400.
\end{aligned}
\label{eq:ordering-conditioned-gap}$$ These bounds are for the conditioned $P_0$ tuples. Applying <a href="#eq:ordering-slot-permutation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ordering-slot-permutation">[eq:ordering-slot-permutation]</a> makes every occurrence use the original predicate $\Pi$ with exactly the same values. Call this emitted instance $I$.

We now make the choices for the prescribed $\delta$. Choose a positive rational $\eta$ with $10\eta<\delta$, and a rational $0<\kappa<1/2$ with $2\kappa k<\eta$. Put $K=\binom{k}{2}$. Choose an integer $q$ so large that $$\frac1q\le\mu_0(\kappa),\qquad Kq^{-\kappa/2}<\eta.$$ Choose the data $J,m,\alpha$ from Lemma <a href="#lem:ordering-local-data" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:ordering-local-data">31</a> at error $\eta$. The two analytic error functions now have fixed parameters and are uniform in $R$. Choose a positive rational $t<1/(kq)$ so small that $$Kq r_c(t)<\eta,\qquad r_s(kqt)<\eta.$$ Set $\chi=\eta$. Choose positive rationals $\zeta,\nu<1$ with $$\zeta<\eta,\qquad
 \nu<\frac{\eta\kappa t^4}{6400},$$ and choose an integer $H\ge\max\{k,q\}$ with $K/H<\eta$. These choices depend only on $k,\Pi,\delta$. In particular, they precede both the choice of $h$ in Lemma <a href="#lem:simple-strong-left" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:simple-strong-left">29</a> and the alphabet supplied by Theorem <a href="#thm:ugc" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:ugc">1</a>.

For every $h\ge H$, $\delta_h<\eta$. The local gap <a href="#eq:ordering-local-gap" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ordering-local-gap">[eq:ordering-local-gap]</a>, Bernoulli’s inequality, and <a href="#eq:ordering-conditioned-gap" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ordering-conditioned-gap">[eq:ordering-conditioned-gap]</a> give $$\mathrm{YES}:\quad
 \mathop{\mathrm{OPT}}_\Pi(I)\ge1-\zeta-2\kappa k-\eta-\delta_h
 >1-4\eta>1-\delta,$$ whereas <a href="#eq:ordering-pseudorandom-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ordering-pseudorandom-bound">[eq:ordering-pseudorandom-bound]</a> gives $$\mathrm{NO}:\quad
 \mathop{\mathrm{OPT}}_\Pi(I)\le S_{\mathrm{loc}}+\chi+\delta_h
 <\rho_\Pi+6\eta<\rho_\Pi+\delta.$$ This proves the numerical claims simultaneously for every $R$.

Finally we specify the encoded transformation. Once $R$ is fixed, the choice of $c$, the product $\mu_c^{\otimes R}$, and the independent rational $2\kappa$-noise form one fixed finite rational law $\theta_R$ on local outcomes $\xi$. These are the actual local outcomes above; the Gaussian variables used for the analytic comparison are not part of the output. Renumber $B$ by $[|B|]$ and explicitly list $\mathcal V=B\times[m]^R$. For each $a\in A$, each ordered distinct neighbor tuple, and each $\xi\in\operatorname{supp}\theta_R$, list the tuple in <a href="#eq:ordering-iid-queries" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ordering-iid-queries">[eq:ordering-iid-queries]</a>, permuted by <a href="#eq:ordering-slot-permutation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ordering-slot-permutation">[eq:ordering-slot-permutation]</a>, with weight <a id="eq:ordering-rational-weight"></a>$$\frac{\theta_R(\xi)}{|A|(h)_k}.
\label{eq:ordering-rational-weight}$$ The weights sum to one and are nonnegative rationals. Every listed tuple has distinct IDs; the same tuple may occur in several entries. There are $|B|m^R$ variables and at most $|A|(h)_k|\operatorname{supp}\theta_R|$ entries. For fixed $k,m,R,\theta_R$, these counts are polynomial in the explicit graph size, even when $h$ varies. A fixed common denominator for $\theta_R$ shows that each weight has $O(\log|A|+k\log h)$ bits plus a fixed constant. The IDs have $O(\log|B|)$ bits plus a fixed constant. Exact enumeration of these entries is therefore deterministic and polynomial for fixed $R$, as asserted. ◻

</details>

The same occurrence-list encoding also justifies the deterministic random-order guarantee in Section <a href="#sec:consequences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:consequences">8</a>. Build an order by exposing one next variable at a time. If exactly $s$ variables of a constraint have been exposed, its conditional success probability is the number of permutations in $\Pi$ whose first $s$ positions are the roles of those variables in the exposed order, divided by $(k-s)!$. It can be computed by inspecting the fixed set $S_k$. For each choice of the next variable, sum these probabilities against the listed rational weights and choose a next variable whose conditional expectation is at least the average over the choices. This preserves the initial expectation $|\Pi|/k!$. All comparisons are exact and polynomial in the input bit length: the product of the weight denominators has bit length at most their total encoded length, and the factorial denominators have constant size for fixed $k$. The variable list is explicit, so the number of prefix steps and candidate choices is polynomial as well.

<a id="pm-h-47"></a>
#### References

<div id="refs" class="references csl-bib-body hanging-indent">

<div id="ref-ABS10" class="csl-entry">

Arora, Sanjeev, Boaz Barak, and David Steurer. 2010. “Subexponential Algorithms for Unique Games and Related Problems.” In *Proceedings of the 51st Annual IEEE Symposium on Foundations of Computer Science*, 563–72. IEEE Computer Society. <https://doi.org/10.1109/FOCS.2010.59>.

      "caption": "The solid arrows describe the reduction; the dashed arrows describe its soundness argument. The decoding lower bound follows from high test acceptance, while the clean-coordinate upper bound follows from the NO parity gap and applies to every advice strategy. The constants $c, γ > 0$ are fixed before the tuple length $k$ grows.",

      ],
      "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[\n  x=1cm,y=1cm,\n  every node/.style={font=\\small},\n  box/.style={draw=linkblue!80!black,rounded corners=2pt,\n    text width=5.9cm,align=center,inner sep=8pt,minimum height=11mm},\n  branch/.style={box,text width=2.70cm,inner sep=6pt,minimum height=15mm},\n  heading/.style={font=\\small\\bfseries,align=center},\n  reduction/.style={-{Latex[length=2.1mm]},thick,draw=linkblue!80!black},\n  implication/.style={-{Latex[length=2.1mm]},thick,dashed,draw=black!70}\n]\n\\node[heading] at (0,0) {The reduction};\n\\node[heading] at (7.4,0) {Soundness on a NO input};\n\n\\node[box] (parity) at (0,-1.0)\n  {$\\mathrm{3SAT}$\\\\$\\longrightarrow$ weighted parity gap instance};\n\\node[box] (matrix) at (0,-2.8)\n  {Latent matrix test\\\\\n   stable nonlinear map: Lemma~\\ref{lem:latent}};\n\\node[box] (ugc) at (0,-6.2)\n  {Unweighted simple bipartite\\\\Unique Games\\\\\n   Theorem~\\ref{thm:ugc}};\n\\draw[reduction] (parity) -- (matrix);\n\\draw[reduction] (matrix) -- node[midway,fill=white,inner sep=4pt,\n    text width=5.0cm,align=center]\n  {Rounding and subdivision;\\\\parallel repetition} (ugc);\n\n\\node[branch] (assumption) at (5.65,-1.0)\n  {Assume test acceptance\\\\at least $0.99$};\n\\node[branch] (no) at (9.15,-1.0)\n  {NO parity\\\\instance};\n\\node[branch] (decoding) at (5.65,-3.3)\n  {Decoded advice strategies\\\\agreement at least $\\gamma>0$\\\\\n   Proposition~\\ref{prop:matrix-decoding}};\n\\node[branch] (clean) at (9.15,-3.3)\n  {Every pair of\\\\strategies:\\\\agreement at most\\\\$\\exp(-c k^{1/3})$\\\\\n   Lemma~\\ref{lem:clean}};\n\\node[box] (contradiction) at (7.4,-6.2)\n  {Contradiction for sufficiently large $k$:\\\\\n   $\\exp(-c k^{1/3})<\\gamma$};\n\\draw[implication] (assumption) -- (decoding);\n\\draw[implication] (no) -- (clean);\n\\draw[implication] (decoding) -- (contradiction);\n\\draw[implication] (clean) -- (contradiction);\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "A flow diagram arranged in two columns:\nLeft column header: \"The reduction\"\n- Rectangle:\n  3SAT\n  $\\longrightarrow$ weighted parity gap instance\n- Solid downward arrow to rectangle:\n  Latent matrix test\n  stable nonlinear map: Lemma 3.1\n- Solid downward arrow labeled \"Rounding and subdivision; parallel repetition\" to rectangle:\n  Unweighted simple bipartite\n  Unique Games\n  Theorem 1.1\n\nRight column header: \"Soundness on a NO input\"\n- Two rectangles side-by-side:\n  Left:\n  Assume test\n  acceptance\n  at least 0.99\n  Right:\n  NO parity\n  instance\n- Dashed downward arrow from \"Assume test acceptance at least 0.99\" to rectangle:\n  Decoded advice\n  strategies\n  agreement at\n  least $\\gamma > 0$\n  Proposition 5.3\n- Dashed downward arrow from \"NO parity instance\" to rectangle:\n  Every pair of\n  strategies:\n  agreement\n  at most\n  $\\exp(-ck^{1/3})$\n  Lemma 6.2\n- Dashed downward arrows from both \"Decoded advice strategies...\" and \"Every pair of strategies...\" converge to a rounded rectangle:\n  Contradiction for sufficiently large $k$:\n  $\\exp(-ck^{1/3}) < \\gamma$"
    },
    {
      "caption": "One level of the latent recursion, shown under its additive noise. The input space is a product over all child indices $(A,J)$; only one uniformly chosen child is perturbed. Each child output is embedded in $U_{A} ≤ X ⊕ B$, all embedded outputs are added, and the quadratic map is applied once at the parent. Here $(x,y)$ is the initial aggregate, and $Δ$ is the selected child’s output difference, so the noisy aggregate is $(x′,y′) = (x,y) + J_{*}Δ$. The omitted children obey the same rule. The highlighted perturbation may disappear at the parent.",
      "figure_id": "fig:latent-recursion",
      "pdf_page": 13,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "fig:latent-recursion",
          "exact_quote": "One level of the latent recursion, shown under its additive noise.\nThe input space is a product over all child indices $(A,J)$; only one\nuniformly chosen child is perturbed. Each child output is embedded in\n$U_A\\le X\\oplus B$, all embedded ",
          "source_object": "fig:0002"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[\n every node/.style={font=\\small,align=center},\n block/.style={draw=linkblue!80!black,rounded corners=2pt,\n text width=3.3cm,inner sep=7pt,minimum height=14mm},\n flow/.style={-{Latex[length=2mm]},thick}]\n\\node[block] (left) at (0,0)\n {One child input\\\\$u_{AJ}\\in P_{t-1}$\\\\$J C_{t-1}(u_{AJ})$};\n\\node[block,draw=red!70!black] (selected) at (4.8,0)\n {Selected child input\\\\$u_{A_*J_*}+\\xi_{t-1}$\\\\$J_*C_{t-1}(u_{A_*J_*}+\\xi_{t-1})$};\n\\node[block] (right) at (9.6,0)\n {Other child inputs\\\\$u_{AJ}\\in P_{t-1}$\\\\$J C_{t-1}(u_{AJ})$};\n\\node at (2.4,0) {$\\cdots$};\n\\node at (7.2,0) {$\\cdots$};\n\\node[block,text width=7cm] (sum) at (4.8,-2)\n {Sum over every child $(A,J)\\in\\mathcal I$\\\\\n noisy aggregate $(x',y')=(x,y)+J_*\\Delta$};\n\\draw[flow] (left.south) -- (sum.north west);\n\\draw[flow,draw=red!70!black] (selected) -- (sum);\n\\draw[flow] (right.south) -- (sum.north east);\n\\node[block,text width=7cm] (out) at (4.8,-3.8)\n {Noisy parent output $y'+Q(x')\\in B$};\n\\draw[flow] (sum) -- (out);\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "A tree-structured aggregation diagram:\nTop row contains three rectangular boxes:\n- Left box:\n  One child input\n  $u_{AJ} \\in P_{t-1}$\n  $J C_{t-1}(u_{AJ})$\n- Horizontal dots $\\cdots$\n- Middle box (shaded/tinted):\n  Selected child input\n  $u_{A_* J_*} + \\xi_{t-1}$\n  $J_* C_{t-1}(u_{A_* J_*} + \\xi_{t-1})$\n- Horizontal dots $\\cdots$\n- Right box:\n  Other child inputs\n  $u_{AJ} \\in P_{t-1}$\n  $J C_{t-1}(u_{AJ})$\n\nArrows from all three top boxes point down into a middle rectangular box:\n- Middle box:\n  Sum over every child $(A, J) \\in \\mathcal{I}$\n  noisy aggregate $(x', y') = (x, y) + J_* \\Delta$\n\nA downward arrow from the middle box points to a bottom rectangular box:\n- Bottom box:\n  Noisy parent output $y' + Q(x') \\in B$"
    },
    {
      "caption": "The two decoders use independent random tapes. Neither receives the sampled $M_{O}$ or the projection $π̄$; the right decoder chooses its own representative $M_{0}$ from the visible advice. Every displayed advice map includes its affine intercept, through the homogeneous coordinate. The transferred slice is used only to analyze these private rules: the right decoder does not use the left witness or answer set. If the left advice is not good, or if $σ|_{H} = 0$ on the right, that decoder returns an arbitrary valid answer.",

          "source_object": "fig:0003"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[\n  x=1cm,y=1cm,\n  every node/.style={font=\\small},\n  box/.style={draw=linkblue!80!black,rounded corners=2pt,\n    text width=6.0cm,align=center,inner sep=8pt},\n  input/.style={box,minimum height=16mm},\n  rule/.style={box,minimum height=35mm},\n  output/.style={box,minimum height=10mm},\n  flow/.style={-{Latex[length=2.1mm]},thick,draw=black!70}\n]\n\\node[box,text width=13.3cm,minimum height=12mm] (sample) at (3.7,0)\n  {\\textbf{Referee}\\\\[2pt]\n   Sample $U,O,\\bar\\pi,A,T_O,M_O$; set $S_O=AM_O$.\n   Send only the inputs below.};\n\n\\node[input] (uinput) at (0,-1.9)\n  {\\textbf{Equation decoder}\\\\\n   $(U,A,T_U,S_U)$\\\\\n   $T_U=T_O\\bar\\pi,\\quad S_U=S_O\\bar\\pi$};\n\\node[input] (oinput) at (7.4,-1.9)\n  {\\textbf{Projected decoder}\\\\\n   $(O,A,T_O,S_O)$};\n\\draw[flow] (sample.south -| uinput.north) -- (uinput.north);\n\\draw[flow] (sample.south -| oinput.north) -- (oinput.north);\n\n\\node[rule] (urule) at (0,-4.8)\n  {Choose the fixed slice witness and\\\\its set of valid answers\\\\[2pt]\n   $\\mathcal A=(z+Z)\\cap\\{v:e_0^\\top v=1\\}$\\\\\n   from this decoder's visible advice.\\\\[6pt]\n   \\textit{Private randomness:}\\\\\n   sample $a_U$ uniformly from $\\mathcal A$.};\n\\node[rule] (orule) at (7.4,-4.8)\n  {Set $H=\\ker A$. Choose $M_0$\\\\with $AM_0=S_O$.\\\\[5pt]\n   \\textit{Private randomness:}\\\\\n   sample uniform $\\sigma\\in K^*$, then $\\Phi$\\\\\n   with probability $|\\widehat f_\\sigma(\\Phi)|^2$.\\\\[5pt]\n   If $\\sigma|_H\\ne0$, use a fixed $h\\in H$\\\\\n   with $\\sigma(h)=1$; set $a_O=\\Phi(h)$.};\n\\draw[flow] (uinput) -- (urule);\n\\draw[flow] (oinput) -- (orule);\n\n\\node[output] (uoutput) at (0,-7.9)\n  {$a_U\\in L_U,\\qquad e_0^\\top a_U=1$};\n\\node[output] (ooutput) at (7.4,-7.9)\n  {$a_O\\in L_O,\\qquad e_0^\\top a_O=1$};\n\\draw[flow] (urule) -- (uoutput);\n\\draw[flow] (orule) -- (ooutput);\n\n\\node[box,text width=8.2cm,minimum height=11mm] (verify) at (3.7,-9.6)\n  {\\textbf{Referee checks}\\quad $\\bar\\pi a_U=a_O$};\n\\draw[flow] (uoutput.south) -- (verify.north -| uoutput.south);\n\\draw[flow] (ooutput.south) -- (verify.north -| ooutput.south);\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "Flowchart diagram of the referee and two decoders:\nTop rectangular box:\n- Referee\n  Sample $U, O, \\bar{\\pi}, A, \\bar{T}_O, M_O$; set $S_O = A M_O$. Send only the inputs below.\n\nTwo downward arrows lead to two side-by-side rectangular decoder boxes:\n- Left decoder box:\n  Equation decoder\n  $(U, A, T_U, S_U)$\n  $T_U = \\bar{T}_O \\bar{\\pi}, \\quad S_U = S_O \\bar{\\pi}$\n- Right decoder box:\n  Projected decoder\n  $(O, A, T_O, S_O)$\n\nDownward arrow from Equation decoder to rectangular box:\n- Choose the fixed slice witness and\n  its set of valid answers\n  $\\mathcal{A} = (z + Z) \\cap \\{v : e_0^\\top v = 1\\}$\n  from this decoder's visible advice.\n  Private randomness:\n  sample $a_U$ uniformly from $\\mathcal{A}$.\n\nDownward arrow from Projected decoder to rectangular box:\n- Set $H = \\ker A$. Choose $M_0$\n  with $A M_0 = S_O$.\n  Private randomness:\n  sample uniform $\\sigma \\in K^*$, then $\\Phi$\n  with probability $|\\widehat{f}_\\sigma(\\Phi)|^2$.\n  If $\\sigma|_H \\ne 0$, use a fixed $h \\in H$\n  with $\\sigma(h) = 1$; set $a_O = \\Phi(h)$.\n\nDownward arrow from left slice witness box to rectangular box:\n- $a_U \\in L_U, \\quad e_0^\\top a_U = 1$\n\nDownward arrow from right decoder actions box to rectangular box:\n- $a_O \\in L_O, \\quad e_0^\\top a_O = 1$\n\nDownward arrows from both $a_U$ and $a_O$ boxes converge to a rectangular box at the bottom:\n- Referee checks $\\bar{\\pi} a_U = a_O$"
    }
  ],
